# AI Media Scout — 2026-11-02

Today completes the new weekly source-review cycle for all 1,512 published tools across every tier, platform and host: 1,512 reviewed, zero untouched entries remaining. This is a dated, bounded source review, not a hands-on or exhaustive dependency audit. Seventeen earlier Experimental labels moved to Quality unverified: five current reasons no longer justify the overall label and twelve could not be confirmed from available current sources. Missing current evidence is not proof of poor quality. The current library retains three Recommended and four Ready to try tools within their specific reviewed workflows; other gaps remain explicit. The weekly batch reopened 122 checks and closed two. GitHub API quota failures, unavailable repositories, Codeberg challenge pages, GitLab 429 responses and failed supplementary URLs are archived; affected checks remain unknown rather than being counted as passed. Rushcut now has an AGPL-3.0-or-later code notice with a narrow VEGAS permission, while its earlier MIT releases remain historical. Twenty-five earlier screened tools now receive complete installation/use/requirements guides: no new tool is added solely on metadata. The guides span kinetic art, diagrams, speech, film, mobile/robot avatars, fabrication, Gaussian splats and game assets. They distinguish software grants, model rights, proprietary hosts and paid services. All platforms receive equal research treatment, with compatibility stated only where documented. No discovered app was installed or executed. New repository candidates remain private when a complete current README/license chain could not be obtained; four selected public-page attempts also failed. The complete retained catalog, including pilot discoveries, remains available for later reviews; there is no fixed daily profile quota. Verified collection: 672/672 repository queries attempted; 672 successful, 0 partial, 0 failed and 121 bounded/truncated. All 18 model tasks were attempted; 0 failed. These are bounded discovery attempts, not an exhaustive census or quality approval. Separate from successful search queries, 537 watchlist fetches and 1231 enrichment attempts failed; the detailed warnings remain in the report. A 404/409 is an availability gap, not proof of abandonment or license revocation.

Documentation reviewed today. Tools are not hands-on tested unless explicitly stated. Requirements and performance remain source-specific.

## Search and review counts

81 creative fields; 672/672 repository queries attempted; 18/18 model-task queries attempted. 28212 distinct source candidates and 862 model leads. 25 detailed profiles and 0 additional screened discoveries. Source gaps: 0 failed repository queries, 0 partial repository queries, 121 bounded repository queries, 0 failed model-task queries and 1 web ecosystem gaps. Raw search candidates include duplicates of known tools, excluded projects and projects awaiting review; they are not verified recommendations.

## Research beyond GitHub

- **gitlab** · searched: Current official GitLab sources were reviewed for EmbeddedAmpModel, AudioStellar, LibreTerrain and OpenPixels, including full software grants. OpenDub/OpenSlides raw/API requests later returned 429 and their websites failed SSL; these explicit gaps do not imply absent projects or revoked historical grants. AudioStellar’s current default is the JUCE/CMake branch, with macOS build work still pending. [Source](https://gitlab.com/)
- **codeberg** · gap: Imaginer and Raspberry Pi Diffusion URLs returned HTTP 200 challenge pages rather than usable source. These were recorded as failed evidence attempts; no compatibility, grant or quality result was inferred. [Source](https://codeberg.org/Imaginer/Imaginer) [Source](https://codeberg.org/)
- **sourcehut** · searched: The official rockerBOO/Diffuser README, full MIT license and Cargo/source text were read. Its absolute local diffusers-rs path prevents an unchanged portable checkout, so setup remains failed. A separate web discovery attempt was robots-limited and was not bypassed. [Source](https://git.sr.ht/~rockerBOO/Diffuser)
- **packages** · searched: Deli Slicer’s official package documentation and project README describe managed Python installation, embedded slicer versions and platform-specific requirements. Package availability does not establish successful printer output; qsys packages similarly remain separate from the proprietary Designer host. [Source](https://pypi.org/project/deli-slicer/) [Source](https://github.com/reowens/qsys-tools)
- **creative-plugins** · searched: Both separately licensed ComfyUI-Idol implementations, JustAE Flow and other current integrations were source-reviewed. Native/custom node paths, model downloads, wrapper code grants and third-party model terms were kept distinct. This is not a claim of exhaustive registry coverage. [Source](https://github.com/Brie-Wensleydale/ComfyUI-Idol) [Source](https://github.com/PedroMarinhoDev/ComfyUI-Idol)
- **project-sites** · searched: Current primary-source attempts cover all 81 observed fields through 73 distinct URLs: 71 successful HTTP fetches and two failed. Some successful bodies are limited navigation/extracts or bounded PDFs; no access-success count is treated as source qualification. [Source](https://stitchfoundry.app/) [Source](https://eagle-animation.com/) [Source](https://www.imprimatur.fyi/)
- **research-code** · searched: MotionBricks’ Unity bridge and the current primary evaluations for Depth Anything V2, SAM 2.1 and Whisper were reviewed with checkpoint/task boundaries. SoundPlot remains outside eligible additions because a released qualifying AI implementation and complete grant were not established. [Source](https://github.com/ninja9998877/motionbricks-unity-bridge) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.12752) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2212.04356)
- **international** · searched: Chinese, Japanese, Russian/English and Thai-origin current documentation was reviewed for creator workflows including Comic Factory, zumen, Oddments and MatePet. Language and research origin never substitute for quality checks or supported-platform evidence. [Source](https://github.com/meta-taro/zumen) [Source](https://github.com/peopleskynet-boop/Oddments) [Source](https://github.com/napatkitiprasanpong/MatePetTemi)

## The Garden’s Response

Physical, robotic & kinetic installations · Interactive, immersive & live media · AI robotic painting and drawing

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Studying embodied audience interaction and adapting a camera-driven kinetic garden, with an inspectable host/dashboard/node architecture.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-02. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/dattodigm/DATT3700/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The maintainer documents a public course showcase and real installation photographs. The architecture distinguishes connected DeepFace/ViT paths from reserved MediaPipe, VLM and early Wekinator concepts. These are owner reports, not independent classifier or exhibition reliability evaluations. [Source](https://github.com/dattodigm/DATT3700/blob/main/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Obtain the source, prepare a Python environment, install python_host/requirements.txt and launch the host. Hardware nodes require their corresponding firmware and wiring; the browser dashboard alone does not supply a finished physical sculpture. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/dattodigm/DATT3700/blob/main/README.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** Project MIT covers its source. DeepFace component models, ViT weights, libraries, photographs and firmware components require separate terms review. Local compute and separately sourced installation hardware; no mandatory paid hosted AI route is documented. [Source](https://github.com/dattodigm/DATT3700/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Facial-expression labels are uncertain model outputs, not measurements of a person’s internal emotional state. Reserved MediaPipe/VLM/feedback-training ideas are not shipped capabilities. The author reports host overheating and backup-camera/computer recovery; no unattended exhibition or physical-safety certification is established.

### How it uses AI

A kinetic-flower installation connects DeepFace and a ViT facial-expression classifier to a Python/Flask host and distributed ESP32 motor, servo, eye-display and light nodes. OpenCV tracking and a damped state machine connect camera observations to OSC or serial movement. [Source](https://github.com/dattodigm/DATT3700/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A kinetic-flower installation connects DeepFace and a ViT facial-expression classifier to a Python/Flask host and distributed ESP32 motor, servo, eye-display and light nodes. OpenCV tracking and a damped state machine connect camera observations to OSC or serial movement. [Source 1](https://github.com/dattodigm/DATT3700/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Studying embodied audience interaction and adapting a camera-driven kinetic garden, with an inspectable host/dashboard/node architecture. [Source 1](https://github.com/dattodigm/DATT3700/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The maintainer documents a public course showcase and real installation photographs. The architecture distinguishes connected DeepFace/ViT paths from reserved MediaPipe, VLM and early Wekinator concepts. These are owner reports, not independent classifier or exhibition reliability evaluations. [Source 1](https://github.com/dattodigm/DATT3700/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Obtain the source, prepare a Python environment, install python_host/requirements.txt and launch the host. Hardware nodes require their corresponding firmware and wiring; the browser dashboard alone does not supply a finished physical sculpture. [Source 1](https://github.com/dattodigm/DATT3700/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Obtain the source, prepare a Python environment, install python_host/requirements.txt and launch the host. Hardware nodes require their corresponding firmware and wiring; the browser dashboard alone does not supply a finished physical sculpture.

```sh
python -m pip install -r python_host/requirements.txt
```


```sh
python -m python_host.main --port 15000
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/dattodigm/DATT3700/blob/main/README.md)

1. Open the host dashboard, choose a camera and confirm tracking before connecting moving hardware.
2. Identify the correct flower-node firmware and configure discovery/gateway or USB serial.
3. Inspect expression confidence, thresholds, decay and cooldown; map stable states to bounded motion.
4. Record/replay a small motion sequence and inspect tracking and actuator behavior before audience use.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/dattodigm/DATT3700/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Camera-capable host plus the selected ESP32 boards, Wi-Fi/USB connection, appropriate power/motor drivers, motors/servos and eye/LED hardware. RAM, VRAM, storage and universal actuator specifications are not documented.
- **Software:** Python, Flask, OpenCV, project requirements, DeepFace and the separately downloaded yst007/vit-emotion model; ESP32 firmware toolchains and display/motor libraries depend on the chosen node. Exact Python version and complete cross-platform matrix are not documented.
- **Platforms:** Host platform matrix not documented; the author reports a backup MacBook in the exhibition.; ESP32 nodes are project-specific hardware; this is not a browser-only installation.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/dattodigm/DATT3700/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/dattodigm/DATT3700/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** Project MIT covers its source. DeepFace component models, ViT weights, libraries, photographs and firmware components require separate terms review.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. Project MIT covers its source. DeepFace component models, ViT weights, libraries, photographs and firmware components require separate terms review.
- **Cost:** Local compute and separately sourced installation hardware; no mandatory paid hosted AI route is documented.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/dattodigm/DATT3700/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Facial-expression labels are uncertain model outputs, not measurements of a person’s internal emotional state. Reserved MediaPipe/VLM/feedback-training ideas are not shipped capabilities. The author reports host overheating and backup-camera/computer recovery; no unattended exhibition or physical-safety certification is established. [Source 1](https://github.com/dattodigm/DATT3700/blob/main/README.md)

### How it uses AI

A local Windows pipeline asks an H3 video model to imagine an orbit around a photo, estimates an AnySplat scene, carves/refines it with gsplat and serves an interactive three.js/Spark viewer with optional WebXR. [Source](https://github.com/dtaddis/Lookaround/blob/main/README.md)

A text-to-diagram language lets an AI coding agent specify exact box positions and line routes while a deterministic compiler checks geometry and renders SVG. ```


```sh
kashika check hello.kash
```


```sh

```


```sh
kashika render hello.kash -o hello.svg
```


```sh

```

### How it uses AI

An audio-reactive room-lighting app combines conventional pitch/onset detection with an optional local EfficientAT neural sound gate. The AI gate distinguishes bowl-like sounds from voices, drums and gongs; it is off by default. [Source](https://github.com/leopardcodeai/chakraa/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

An audio-reactive room-lighting app combines conventional pitch/onset detection with an optional local EfficientAT neural sound gate. The AI gate distinguishes bowl-like sounds from voices, drums and gongs; it is off by default. [Source 1](https://github.com/leopardcodeai/chakraa/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Prototyping sound-to-color installations and comparing a local neural gate with deterministic tone mapping in the Mac/Linux simulator. [Source 1](https://github.com/leopardcodeai/chakraa/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The owner documents real-bowl simulator recordings and a79-clip gate study. The gate reduces unwanted colors while also suppressing some bells/bowls; the small owner-labelled study is not an independent quality or health evaluation. [Source 1](https://github.com/leopardcodeai/chakraa/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Arrange the project beside Espressif’s esp-mosaico-vibe workspace as documented. Use macOS/Linux Python3 and FFmpeg for the simulator; the optional gate has its own local model/dependency setup. [Source 1](https://github.com/leopardcodeai/chakraa/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Arrange the project beside Espressif’s esp-mosaico-vibe workspace as documented. Use macOS/Linux Python3 and FFmpeg for the simulator; the optional gate has its own local model/dependency setup.

```sh
./sim.sh --mic
```


```sh
python3 tools/chakra_feed.py demo
```


```sh
python3 tools/chakra_feed.py mic --ai
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/leopardcodeai/chakraa/blob/main/README.md)

1. Start the simulator and inspect the seven-tone demonstration.
2. Try microphone input without AI, then explicitly enable the optional local AI gate.
3. Learn the actual tones of the chosen bowls and compare false triggers/false rejections.
4. Use a screen-lamp preview before separately configuring real lights or board hardware.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/leopardcodeai/chakraa/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Mac/Linux microphone-capable host for simulation. Proposed board: ESP-Mosaico ESP32-S31 with2.16-inch480×480 display/microphone. The gate’s roughly1.9MB quantized on-board estimate and30–60ms inference estimate are unmeasured; host RAM/VRAM minima are not specified.
- **Software:** macOS/Linux, Python3, FFmpeg and Espressif’s workspace for the simulator; ESP32-S31/ESP-DL tooling for the proposed device route. AI-gate Python/model dependencies are separate from baseline tone detection.
- **Platforms:** macOS and Linux simulator route is documented.; ESP-Mosaico ESP32-S31 firmware builds, but the author says the board is still on order and untested.; Windows simulator support is not documented.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/leopardcodeai/chakraa/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/leopardcodeai/chakraa/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** Apache-2.0 covers project source. EfficientAT weights and the trained head/recording inputs require separate review; testing recordings do not ship with the code. Optional room-light services/hardware retain their own terms.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. Apache-2.0 covers project source. EfficientAT weights and the trained head/recording inputs require separate review; testing recordings do not ship with the code. Optional room-light services/hardware retain their own terms.
- **Cost:** Local compute and optional separately purchased board/lamps; no mandatory cloud inference is documented.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/leopardcodeai/chakraa/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

The selected simulator workflow includes an actual optional AI path; ordinary pitch detection alone is not labelled AI. Estimated on-board model latency/memory is unmeasured. Chakra names/colors are artistic/cultural mappings, not medical measurements or evidence of healing. Real-lamp flicker compliance is not established. [Source 1](https://github.com/leopardcodeai/chakraa/blob/main/README.md)

Scope: Making fast object-splat studies from one generated image or photograph and inspecting the actual crop and imagined hidden sides.

[Source](https://github.com/Brie-Wensleydale/ComfyUI-Idol/blob/main/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Clone into ComfyUI/custom_nodes, install the two documented packages with ComfyUI’s interpreter and --no-deps, restart and open the simple/turntable example graph. Local inference and storage; no mandatory paid generation API is documented. [Source](https://github.com/Brie-Wensleydale/ComfyUI-Idol/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Unseen surfaces are invented; wide orbits can reveal holes/smears, thin structures disappear, and diffuse-only color does not represent glass/metals faithfully. A splat lacks mesh normals, UVs and rigging. Lowering Gaussian count does not reduce the reported peak VRAM.

### How it uses AI

A ComfyUI node pack wraps the Idol image-to-3D Gaussian model and returns a native SPLAT, a preprocessed reference and an optional PLY path. Core ComfyUI nodes handle turntables and export. [Source](https://github.com/Brie-Wensleydale/ComfyUI-Idol/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

- **Software:** Python3.10+ and a compatible ComfyUI with native SPLAT support; tensordict and plyfile. Use the actual ComfyUI interpreter; the shown embedded-Python command is a Windows-portable example.

```sh
corepack enable
```


```sh

```


```sh

```

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/StefanMenne/gb10-assets/blob/main/LICENSE)

Reported video length limits are measurements on one 128 GB GB10 system, not universal limits or output-quality scores. [Source](https://github.com/StefanMenne/gb10-assets/blob/main/README.md)

Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/StefanMenne/gb10-assets/blob/main/README.md)

FLUX.2 klein 4B, Qwen image edit and Qwen TTS routes are documented as Apache-2.0; Pixal3D includes separately licensed DINOv3. Optional LTX-2.5 is under a gated Community license with a revenue condition, not an open-source model grant. Blender/ComfyUI remain GPL components. Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate. [Source](https://github.com/StefanMenne/gb10-assets/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Only one model stays resident at a time. The service can execute submitted Blender Python inside its container, so token holders have broader authority than generation alone. The dashboard is visible to reachable LAN clients. Do not transfer GB10 memory/time figures to desktop GPUs.

### How it uses AI

A self-hosted REST/MCP service orchestrates local image, edit, speech, video and 3D generation on NVIDIA GB10 machines, returning assets to a remote creative workstation. [Source](https://github.com/StefanMenne/gb10-assets/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A self-hosted REST/MCP service orchestrates local image, edit, speech, video and 3D generation on NVIDIA GB10 machines, returning assets to a remote creative workstation. [Source 1](https://github.com/StefanMenne/gb10-assets/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Queue game-prop images and voice lines on a separate DGX Spark or Ascent GX10 while editing on a client workstation. [Source 1](https://github.com/StefanMenne/gb10-assets/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README shows the server dashboard and image/video studios with example job commands. Reported video length limits are measurements on one 128 GB GB10 system, not universal limits or output-quality scores. [Source 1](https://github.com/StefanMenne/gb10-assets/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

On GB10 install Docker Compose and NVIDIA Container Toolkit, clone and initialize the authenticated configuration, build and start the image, then install only chosen model tools. The server image is linux/arm64 with CUDA 13. A client uses uv tool install for gb10-assets[client] and a configured server address/token. [Source 1](https://github.com/StefanMenne/gb10-assets/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. On GB10 install Docker Compose and NVIDIA Container Toolkit, clone and initialize the authenticated configuration, build and start the image, then install only chosen model tools. The server image is linux/arm64 with CUDA 13. A client uses uv tool install for gb10-assets[client] and a configured server address/token.

```sh
scripts/docker.sh init
```


```sh
scripts/docker.sh build
```


```sh
scripts/docker.sh up
```


```sh
scripts/docker.sh install flux2-klein
```


```sh
uv tool install "gb10-assets[client] @ git+https://github.com/StefanMenne/gb10-assets"
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/StefanMenne/gb10-assets/blob/main/README.md)

1. Start with the FLUX.2 klein 4B image route and check the dashboard for installed-tool state.
2. Submit a short image job from the client and inspect the output before chaining it into a game asset.
3. Install another model only after checking its license, storage footprint and memory limits.

- **Hardware:** The server image is about 1.2 GB; FLUX.2 klein plus environment about 21 GB, all tools about 160 GB. README model weights range from about 6 to 59 GB. Video measurements use a GB10 with 128 GB unified memory; no universal minimum is stated.
- **Software:** NVIDIA GB10 Linux ARM64/CUDA 13, Docker Compose and NVIDIA Container Toolkit; uv on the client. Linux ARM64 bpy is built from source; Blender build additionally requires compilers and EGL headers.
- **Platforms:** NVIDIA GB10 Linux ARM64 server; Remote client host support is not fully enumerated

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Bake short pose or path-driven character clips on an NVIDIA host, then ship playback without mobile-device inference.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-02. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/ninja9998877/motionbricks-unity-bridge/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The owner records a November 1 RTX 3060 first-boot pass and later Clip Baker pass with Timeline DefaultMale. These owner checks are not broad retargeting or animation-quality validation. [Source](https://github.com/ninja9998877/motionbricks-unity-bridge/blob/main/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Set up the official MotionBricks uv environment on an NVIDIA GPU host with Python 3.12, CUDA 12 and TensorRT. Run the bridge server inside that environment with injected FastAPI/uvicorn. Copy runtime C# files into a Unity 2021.3+ project and editor files under an Editor folder. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/ninja9998877/motionbricks-unity-bridge/blob/main/README.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** Apache-2.0 covers the bridge. Official MotionBricks code is separately Apache-2.0; pretrained ONNX weights use NVIDIA Open Model License with attribution/trustworthy-AI conditions. Unity and imported character assets have separate terms. Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate. [Source](https://github.com/ninja9998877/motionbricks-unity-bridge/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Retargeting is approximate and can slide feet or intersect bodies, especially with different proportions. The model is kinematic, not physics validated. A request generates roughly 0.6–1.9 seconds; chaining and LAN latency affect control. Generic transform clips assume normalized root placement.

### How it uses AI

A Python inference server drives Unity Humanoid characters using NVIDIA MotionBricks; an editor baker converts generated motion into ordinary .anim clips for offline game playback. [Source](https://github.com/ninja9998877/motionbricks-unity-bridge/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A Python inference server drives Unity Humanoid characters using NVIDIA MotionBricks; an editor baker converts generated motion into ordinary .anim clips for offline game playback. [Source 1](https://github.com/ninja9998877/motionbricks-unity-bridge/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Bake short pose or path-driven character clips on an NVIDIA host, then ship playback without mobile-device inference. [Source 1](https://github.com/ninja9998877/motionbricks-unity-bridge/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The owner records a November 1 RTX 3060 first-boot pass and later Clip Baker pass with Timeline DefaultMale. These owner checks are not broad retargeting or animation-quality validation. [Source 1](https://github.com/ninja9998877/motionbricks-unity-bridge/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Set up the official MotionBricks uv environment on an NVIDIA GPU host with Python 3.12, CUDA 12 and TensorRT. Run the bridge server inside that environment with injected FastAPI/uvicorn. Copy runtime C# files into a Unity 2021.3+ project and editor files under an Editor folder. [Source 1](https://github.com/ninja9998877/motionbricks-unity-bridge/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Set up the official MotionBricks uv environment on an NVIDIA GPU host with Python 3.12, CUDA 12 and TensorRT. Run the bridge server inside that environment with injected FastAPI/uvicorn. Copy runtime C# files into a Unity 2021.3+ project and editor files under an Editor folder.

```sh
uv run --with fastapi --with uvicorn python /path/to/motionbricks-unity-bridge/server/motionbricks_server.py --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/ninja9998877/motionbricks-unity-bridge/blob/main/README.md)

1. Verify /info and /health on the inference server.
2. Use a Humanoid Avatar with an empty Animator controller and validate the T-pose mirror mapping.
3. Configure a short Clip Baker take, bake the .anim and inspect foot contact, root motion and proportions before shipping.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/ninja9998877/motionbricks-unity-bridge/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** An NVIDIA GPU is required for inference; an owner RTX 3060 pass is documented but VRAM/RAM/storage minima are not. The mobile game consumes baked clips without running this model.
- **Software:** Python 3.12, NVIDIA CUDA 12/TensorRT and the official MotionBricks uv environment. Unity 2021.3+ with a Humanoid Avatar; first startup builds a cached TensorRT plan.
- **Platforms:** NVIDIA CUDA host; host OS not specified by this bridge; Unity editor/client; Mobile playback through offline baked clips, not on-device inference

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/ninja9998877/motionbricks-unity-bridge/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/ninja9998877/motionbricks-unity-bridge/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** Apache-2.0 covers the bridge. Official MotionBricks code is separately Apache-2.0; pretrained ONNX weights use NVIDIA Open Model License with attribution/trustworthy-AI conditions. Unity and imported character assets have separate terms.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. Apache-2.0 covers the bridge. Official MotionBricks code is separately Apache-2.0; pretrained ONNX weights use NVIDIA Open Model License with attribution/trustworthy-AI conditions. Unity and imported character assets have separate terms.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/ninja9998877/motionbricks-unity-bridge/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Retargeting is approximate and can slide feet or intersect bodies, especially with different proportions. The model is kinematic, not physics validated. A request generates roughly 0.6–1.9 seconds; chaining and LAN latency affect control. Generic transform clips assume normalized root placement. [Source 1](https://github.com/ninja9998877/motionbricks-unity-bridge/blob/main/README.md)

## MatePet for temi

Avatars, digital humans & lip sync · AI robotic painting and drawing · Interactive, immersive & live media

The repository was created on 2026-10-28. This is its first full guide in this library, not independent validation of a new release.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Create an English-speaking embodied character overlay and prototype an exhibition robot companion without cloud conversation inference.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-02. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/napatkitiprasanpong/MatePetTemi/blob/main/LICENSE)

Some transparency behavior remains untested on the physical robot; no independent avatar/voice acceptance was established. [Source](https://github.com/napatkitiprasanpong/MatePetTemi/blob/main/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Open the Android project in Android Studio, allow its pinned llama.cpp/NDK and CMake 3.22.1 setup, build and ADB-install the APK. Open the robot ADB port and use the asset installer to place the local model, Vosk voice model and licensed VRM; grant microphone and overlay permissions. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/napatkitiprasanpong/MatePetTemi/blob/main/README.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.

- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT covers the application. SmolLM2 is credited under Apache-2.0; llama.cpp, Filament, temi SDK and the selected Vosk model retain separate terms. Each VRM has its own license; no avatar rights are conferred by this code. Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate. [Source](https://github.com/napatkitiprasanpong/MatePetTemi/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. No conversation memory; the small model can be wrong. Commands such as mission control are rule-matched, not LLM reasoning. Android 11 microphone sharing may silence wake-word capture; open once after reboot. MToon shading is unsupported and transparency was not physically tested.

### How it uses AI

An Android overlay combines a VRM avatar, Vosk speech recognition and local SmolLM2 conversation on a temi robot, with speech voiced by temi itself. [Source](https://github.com/napatkitiprasanpong/MatePetTemi/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

An Android overlay combines a VRM avatar, Vosk speech recognition and local SmolLM2 conversation on a temi robot, with speech voiced by temi itself. [Source 1](https://github.com/napatkitiprasanpong/MatePetTemi/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Create an English-speaking embodied character overlay and prototype an exhibition robot companion without cloud conversation inference. [Source 1](https://github.com/napatkitiprasanpong/MatePetTemi/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README documents the floating pet and status interface, rendering optimizations and local voice loop. Some transparency behavior remains untested on the physical robot; no independent avatar/voice acceptance was established. [Source 1](https://github.com/napatkitiprasanpong/MatePetTemi/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Open the Android project in Android Studio, allow its pinned llama.cpp/NDK and CMake 3.22.1 setup, build and ADB-install the APK. Open the robot ADB port and use the asset installer to place the local model, Vosk voice model and licensed VRM; grant microphone and overlay permissions. [Source 1](https://github.com/napatkitiprasanpong/MatePetTemi/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Open the Android project in Android Studio, allow its pinned llama.cpp/NDK and CMake 3.22.1 setup, build and ADB-install the APK. Open the robot ADB port and use the asset installer to place the local model, Vosk voice model and licensed VRM; grant microphone and overlay permissions.

```sh
adb install -r app/build/outputs/apk/debug/app-debug.apk
```


```sh
scripts/install-assets.sh <temi-ip> path/to/avatar.vrm
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/napatkitiprasanpong/MatePetTemi/blob/main/README.md)

1. Launch once from the robot launcher and inspect Status for model, voice and avatar state.
2. Say hey mate in a quiet room and test a short dialogue.
3. Check the avatar blink and A/aa expressions; use a smaller VRM if rendering is slow.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/napatkitiprasanpong/MatePetTemi/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Requires a temi robot. The author recommends a VRM about 25 MB or less with textures at most 1024 px; the Vosk model is about 40 MB. Minimum system RAM and complete disk footprint are not documented.
- **Software:** Android Studio, NDK/CMake 3.22.1 and ADB. Targets temi V2 Android 6.0.1/API 23 and V3 Android 11; English Vosk, llama.cpp and Filament.
- **Platforms:** temi V2 Android 6.0.1/API 23; temi V3 Android 11, with microphone-sharing caveat

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/napatkitiprasanpong/MatePetTemi/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/napatkitiprasanpong/MatePetTemi/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT covers the application. SmolLM2 is credited under Apache-2.0; llama.cpp, Filament, temi SDK and the selected Vosk model retain separate terms. Each VRM has its own license; no avatar rights are conferred by this code.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT covers the application. SmolLM2 is credited under Apache-2.0; llama.cpp, Filament, temi SDK and the selected Vosk model retain separate terms. Each VRM has its own license; no avatar rights are conferred by this code.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/napatkitiprasanpong/MatePetTemi/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

No conversation memory; the small model can be wrong. Commands such as mission control are rule-matched, not LLM reasoning. Android 11 microphone sharing may silence wake-word capture; open once after reboot. MToon shading is unsupported and transparency was not physically tested. [Source 1](https://github.com/napatkitiprasanpong/MatePetTemi/blob/main/README.md)

## Mosaic Template Studio

3D printing & generative CAD · Images & design

The repository was created on 2026-10-20. This is its first full guide in this library, not independent validation of a new release.

### Quality assessment: Experimental

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Compare original and AI-enlarged artwork, specify physical dimensions and prepare a calibrated paper template for handmade mosaics.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-02. No local execution implied.

Why Experimental: The current release report explicitly blocks production publication pending physical printer calibration, clean offline-machine installation and artist acceptance, despite implemented software and inference tests. https://github.com/mattkoczwara/template-studio/blob/main/README.md

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/mattkoczwara/template-studio/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The README shows a rendered bilingual WPF editor and describes real CPU/GPU inference and PDF checks. Physical printer measurements and independent artist acceptance remain open; an accepted print job is not a measured physical print. [Source](https://github.com/mattkoczwara/template-studio/blob/main/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** There is no downloadable installer yet. Build on Windows x64 with .NET SDK 10.0.402; dotnet run starts the Release app. Inno Setup 7.1.0 plus Build-Release.ps1 creates a self-contained unsigned installer. The first restore needs Internet; the packaged core workflow includes its AI workers/model and runs offline. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/mattkoczwara/template-studio/blob/main/README.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT covers original project code and presentation assets. CUNet weights, native workers, PDF/runtime components and imported artwork retain separate terms documented in third-party notices; no rights to user artwork are granted. Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate. [Source](https://github.com/mattkoczwara/template-studio/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The current release is a candidate with production publication blocked pending physical printer, clean offline-machine and artist acceptance. It does not segment tesserae or generate cutting paths. AI may change fine lines; borderless printer expansion can crop seams.

### How it uses AI

A Windows mosaic-template editor optionally enlarges artwork with a bundled CUNet AI model, then tiles the result into full-color life-size Letter pages or PDF. [Source](https://github.com/mattkoczwara/template-studio/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A Windows mosaic-template editor optionally enlarges artwork with a bundled CUNet AI model, then tiles the result into full-color life-size Letter pages or PDF. [Source 1](https://github.com/mattkoczwara/template-studio/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Compare original and AI-enlarged artwork, specify physical dimensions and prepare a calibrated paper template for handmade mosaics. [Source 1](https://github.com/mattkoczwara/template-studio/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README shows a rendered bilingual WPF editor and describes real CPU/GPU inference and PDF checks. Physical printer measurements and independent artist acceptance remain open; an accepted print job is not a measured physical print. [Source 1](https://github.com/mattkoczwara/template-studio/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

There is no downloadable installer yet. Build on Windows x64 with .NET SDK 10.0.402; dotnet run starts the Release app. Inno Setup 7.1.0 plus Build-Release.ps1 creates a self-contained unsigned installer. The first restore needs Internet; the packaged core workflow includes its AI workers/model and runs offline. [Source 1](https://github.com/mattkoczwara/template-studio/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. There is no downloadable installer yet. Build on Windows x64 with .NET SDK 10.0.402; dotnet run starts the Release app. Inno Setup 7.1.0 plus Build-Release.ps1 creates a self-contained unsigned installer. The first restore needs Internet; the packaged core workflow includes its AI workers/model and runs offline.

```sh
dotnet run --project src/Mosaic.App/Mosaic.App.csproj -c Release -r win-x64 -p:RestoreLockedMode=true
```


```sh
.\scripts\Build-Release.ps1
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/mattkoczwara/template-studio/blob/main/README.md)

1. Open a JPEG, PNG, WebP or BMP and enter the intended finished dimensions.
2. Compare Standard enlargement with Enhanced CUNet output and preserve the original.
3. Run the four-sheet printer calibration, measure it, then print/export at Actual size or 100 percent and save the .mosaic project.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/mattkoczwara/template-studio/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Windows 10 build 19041+ or Windows 11 x64. .NET SDK 10.0.402 for building; Inno Setup 7.1.0 for the installer. Vulkan driver enables GPU enhancement and a bundled CPU worker provides fallback.
- **Platforms:** Windows 10 build 19041+ x64; Windows 11 x64

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/mattkoczwara/template-studio/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/mattkoczwara/template-studio/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT covers original project code and presentation assets. CUNet weights, native workers, PDF/runtime components and imported artwork retain separate terms documented in third-party notices; no rights to user artwork are granted.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT covers original project code and presentation assets. CUNet weights, native workers, PDF/runtime components and imported artwork retain separate terms documented in third-party notices; no rights to user artwork are granted.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/mattkoczwara/template-studio/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

The current release is a candidate with production publication blocked pending physical printer, clean offline-machine and artist acceptance. It does not segment tesserae or generate cutting paths. AI may change fine lines; borderless printer expansion can crop seams. [Source 1](https://github.com/mattkoczwara/template-studio/blob/main/README.md)

## Excluded and unresolved findings

Research notes only; these do not enter the eligible tool library.

- **New repository source-access gaps** · needs-license-review: Recent metadata surfaced NameIt, Kenshape MCP, Plaque Studio and Stem Player as potentially creative AI work. Their selected public repository reads failed and the core API remains quota-limited; root licenses, exact implementation and requirements were not established. They remain private raw candidates, not eligible screened leads. [Source](https://github.com/nathanlegit/NameIt) [Source](https://github.com/cutetechguy/kenshape-mcp) [Source](https://github.com/kaixuanjun/plaque-studio) [Source](https://github.com/gitguy268/stem-player)
- **SoundPlot** · needs-license-review: The SoundPlot primary paper describes biodiversity soundscape sonification. The inspected account includes PCA/Griffin-Lim processing; a qualifying released AI implementation and complete software license were not established, so it remains a research watchlist item. [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.12752)
- **Eagle Animation AI relevance** · excluded: Eagle Animation’s primary page was researched for frame-by-frame animation. No concrete implemented AI role was established from that page, so an ordinary animation editor is not added merely because it is useful to creators. [Source](https://eagle-animation.com/)
- **ScreenFoundry current alpha** · needs-license-review: Primary product text identifies a screen-print alpha, but a complete source-code grant and concrete implemented AI workflow were not established. The existing StitchFoundry entry does not automatically qualify a different product. [Source](https://stitchfoundry.app/)
- **Dramai modified Apache wording** · needs-license-review: The current complete source grant retains material deviations from the standard Apache body. No new qualifying guide is created from the automated SPDX label. [Source](https://github.com/hyyyyyyz/dramai/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Restricted current code grants** · excluded: Current source reviews retain the separate restrictions on Design OS, OpenSuno YuE2 components and closed Varen product sources. Historical editions and root-license claims do not relicense current components. [Source](https://github.com/vanyu0710/varen-ai-cad) [Source](https://github.com/victorcampeanu/opensuno-yue2)
